- issue-009 (medium)
  - issue: `Collateral is_a Exposure` should be treated as a material semantic modeling error: collateral is a pledged asset securing an exposure, not a subtype of the credit exposure itself.
  - target: Collateral is_a Exposure 선언은 pledged asset을 credit exposure subtype처럼 만들며 Exposure secured_by Collateral relation과 의미가 충돌한다. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml entities.Collateral Source finding context: materialized-input.md:39-46, materialized-input.md:87 Source finding context: Collateral is modeled as an Exposure even though its definition gives it the meaning of a pledged asset. Source finding context: The target itself distinguishes `Exposure` as the loan/credit position and `Collateral` as the securing asset, and the relation `Exposure -> Collateral secured_by` already expresses their association. Treating the securing asset as a subtype of the secured exposure misstates the ontological type and can make inherited exposure semantics apply to collateral records. Source finding context: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure`; keep `Collateral` as a separate entity and express the link only through a relation such as `Exposure secured_by Collateral` or an explicit collateral reference/join entity if needed. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-ea9773ff/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology is intended as a shared concept authority for risk engines and reporting systems. Source finding context: Any consumer that interprets `is_a` as inheritance or subtype membership will treat collateral assets as exposure instances. Source finding context: Risk and reporting consumers may apply exposure attributes, aggregation, or classification assumptions to collateral assets, weakening trust in the shared concept boundary between credit positions and pledged assets. Source finding context: The ontology uses subtype inheritance for a semantic association that is already represented as a securing relation. Source finding context: `Collateral` is declared with `is_a: Exposure`. Source finding context: `Collateral` is defined as an asset securing an exposure, while `Exposure` is defined as the credit exposure itself. Source finding context: The model also declares `Exposure secured_by Collateral`, showing the intended meaning is association rather than subtype identity.
  - failure condition: Any consumer that interprets is_a as inheritance or subtype membership will treat collateral assets as exposure instances. Source finding context: Any consumer that interprets `is_a` as inheritance or subtype membership will treat collateral assets as exposure instances.
  - candidates: fix_now
  - rationale: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure`, keep `Collateral` as a separate entity, and preserve the connection through `Exposure secured_by Collateral` or an explicit join/reference entity if the model needs richer collateral linkage. This should be fixed in the target now because the inherited subtype meaning is the mechanism that can mislead downstream consumers.
  - remediation: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure`, keep `Collateral` as a separate entity, and preserve the connection through `Exposure secured_by Collateral` or an explicit join/reference entity if the model needs richer collateral linkage. This should be fixed in the target now because the inherited subtype meaning is the mechanism that can mislead downstream consumers.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-010 (medium)
  - issue: issue-010은 LTV가 파생 담보비율처럼 명명되고 배치되었지만 독립적인 수동 입력 권위를 가진다는 문제다. formula, collateral selection, valuation/date basis, rule/version authority, override policy, provenance가 없어서 계산된 LTV와 수동 입력 LTV의 의미와 권위가 닫히지 않는다.
  - target: Exposure.ltv는 collateral ratio라는 이름을 갖지만 Exposure.amount와 Collateral.appraised_value 옆에서 formula, valuation basis, timestamp, override semantics 없이 수동 입력된다. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml Exposure.ltv Source finding context: materialized-input.md:29, materialized-input.md:35-37, materialized-input.md:45 Source finding context: `ltv` is named as a collateral ratio but modeled as an independent manually entered input beside its source quantities. Source finding context: A ratio/percentage concept is semantically a derived measure when its source quantities are present in the same concept model. Modeling it as an independent input without defining its formula, valuation basis, timestamp, or override semantics creates a derived-value/input mismatch: the same exposure can have an amount, collateral value, and LTV that do not agree. Source finding context: Define `ltv` as a derived measure with explicit formula, collateral selection rules, valuation date basis, and override policy; if manual underwriting overrides must exist, name them separately, for example `underwriter_ltv_override`, and preserve provenance. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-ea9773ff/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002 Source finding context: The ontology is meant to give risk engines and reports a shared meaning for exposure, limit, and collateral concepts. Source finding context: A consumer compares or recalculates LTV from `Exposure.amount` and `Collateral.appraised_value`, while another consumer trusts the manually entered `Exposure.ltv`. Source finding context: Risk classification and reporting can diverge on the same exposure because the ratio name implies a derived concept but the model gives it independent input authority. Source finding context: The ontology does not distinguish derived LTV from a manually asserted underwriting value. Source finding context: `Exposure.ltv` is a number with a note saying it is manually entered by an underwriter. Source finding context: The same ontology includes `Exposure.amount` and `Collateral.appraised_value`, which are source quantities for a collateral ratio. Source finding context: No formula, valuation basis, or override semantics are declared for reconciling the manually entered value with those source quantities.
  - failure condition: A consumer compares or recalculates LTV from Exposure.amount and Collateral.appraised_value, while another consumer trusts the manually entered Exposure.ltv. Source finding context: A consumer compares or recalculates LTV from `Exposure.amount` and `Collateral.appraised_value`, while another consumer trusts the manually entered `Exposure.ltv`.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: ltv를 formula authority, collateral selection rule, valuation date basis, temporal basis, recalculation rule/version semantics를 가진 derived measure로 정의해야 한다. 수동 underwriting 값이 필요하면 underwriter_ltv_override 같은 별도 concept으로 분리하고 provenance, 승인/정당화, override policy를 붙여야 한다. 이 조치는 issue-006의 broader audit-event gap과 구분되는 LTV-specific authority 문제를 target 내에서 닫기 위한 fix-now 사항이다.
  - remediation: ltv를 formula authority, collateral selection rule, valuation date basis, temporal basis, recalculation rule/version semantics를 가진 derived measure로 정의해야 한다. 수동 underwriting 값이 필요하면 underwriter_ltv_override 같은 별도 concept으로 분리하고 provenance, 승인/정당화, override policy를 붙여야 한다. 이 조치는 issue-006의 broader audit-event gap과 구분되는 LTV-specific authority 문제를 target 내에서 닫기 위한 fix-now 사항이다.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-011 (medium)
  - issue: Issue-011 is a material, focused rating-scale mapping gap: Borrower.internal_rating, RiskRating.grade, and Exposure.risk_grade look like related credit/risk grade concepts, but the ontology does not define their shared authority, scope, conversions, or override semantics.
  - target: Borrower internal rating, RiskRating.grade, and Exposure.risk_grade are shared-looking rating concepts, but their mappings, scopes, manual adjustments, and conversion authority are not defined inside the ontology. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml rating concepts Source finding context: materialized-input.md:20-22, materialized-input.md:31-34, materialized-input.md:48-57, materialized-input.md:92-98 Source finding context: Three credit-rating scales are presented as shared concepts without an authoritative semantic mapping inside the ontology. Source finding context: These names all denote credit/risk grade semantics, but their scopes and transformations are not made authoritative in the shared ontology. A risk engine and a reporting system can therefore use the same ontology while assigning different meanings to equivalent grade levels or treating a manual reporting adjustment as if it were the engine-derived risk grade. Source finding context: Add an explicit canonical rating concept or mapping table in the ontology, including scope (`borrower` vs `exposure`), scale ownership, effective dates, derivation rules, and override/provenance semantics for report-side adjustments. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-ea9773ff/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003 Source finding context: The target says it is a concept authority shared by a risk engine and reporting system. Source finding context: The risk engine emits `RiskRating.grade`, reports consume or adjust `Exposure.risk_grade`, and consumers rely on external or local transformations rather than an ontology-owned mapping. Source finding context: The central risk-grade concept is not semantically stable across systems, so classifications and reports can disagree while both appear conformant to the ontology. Source finding context: The ontology delegates rating-scale equivalence and conversion semantics to external consumer-specific mappings. Source finding context: The ontology defines three rating-like fields with distinct scales: 1-10, R1-R5, and AAA-CCC. Source finding context: The classification rule says `Exposure.risk_grade` is derived from `RiskRating.grade` but can be manually adjusted by the reporting team. Source finding context: The note says the three scales coexist for historical reasons and conversion is performed by each consuming system.
  - failure condition: The risk engine emits RiskRating.grade, reports consume or adjust Exposure.risk_grade, and consumers rely on external or local transformations rather than an ontology-owned mapping. Source finding context: The risk engine emits `RiskRating.grade`, reports consume or adjust `Exposure.risk_grade`, and consumers rely on external or local transformations rather than an ontology-owned mapping.
  - candidates: follow_up
  - rationale: Add an explicit canonical rating concept or ontology-owned mapping table covering borrower versus exposure scope, scale ownership, effective dates, derivation rules, conversion authority, and override/provenance semantics. This should remain linked or subordinate to the broader governed-classification authority work in issue-001, but it needs rating-specific traceability so the risk engine and reports share the same grade semantics.
  - remediation: Add an explicit canonical rating concept or ontology-owned mapping table covering borrower versus exposure scope, scale ownership, effective dates, derivation rules, conversion authority, and override/provenance semantics. This should remain linked or subordinate to the broader governed-classification authority work in issue-001, but it needs rating-specific traceability so the risk engine and reports share the same grade semantics.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
